Between 9 and 10 August 2025, Albanese visited New Zealand prime minister [Christopher Luxon](62303596.md) in [Queenstown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queenstown,_New_Zealand) for an annual bilateral head of government meeting. During the visit, the two heads of government reiterated calls for a ceasefire in the [Gaza war](74998519.md) and condemned Israel's plan to occupy [Gaza City](55387.md).[^cite_note-327] In addition, the two heads of government discussed various bilateral trade, defence issues and Australia's [501 deportation policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migration_Act_1958#2014:_Character_test).[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-Corlett-2025-329] The two leaders also laid [wreaths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wreaths) at a memorial to fallen [ANZAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ANZAC) soldiers in [Arrowtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrowtown) on 10 August.[^cite_note-Corlett-2025-329] On 11 August 2025, Albanese declared that Australia would [formally recognise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_Palestine) [Palestine](241405.md) as a state at the [eightieth session](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eightieth_session_of_the_United_Nations_General_Assembly) of the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md).[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331] In August 2025, the Greens urged the Albanese government to impose direct sanctions on senior members of Netanyahu's government and to stop supplying parts for [F-35 fighter jets](11812.md) to the global supply chain that can be accessed by Israel.[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333]

In 2025, the Albanese government faced increasing pressure to review or suspend arms exports to the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) (UAE) amid allegations that the UAE was providing military support to the [Rapid Support Forces](43053874.md) (RSF) in [Sudan](27421.md), which have been accused of committing [war crimes](74944426.md) and acts of [genocide](75311533.md).[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335] The UAE is Australia's largest export market for weapons and ammunition. Over a five-year period, Australia exported nearly AUD 300 million worth of arms and munitions to the UAE.[^cite_note-336]

In mid-February 2026, Albanese announced that the federal government would not facilitate the repatriation of ISIS-linked families from Syria on security grounds, stating "if you make your bed, you lie in it."[^cite_note-337]

On 28 February 2026, Albanese, in a joint statement with Foreign Minister [Penny Wong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penny_Wong) and Defence Minister [Richard Marles](4386805.md), announced Australia's support for [US and Israeli strikes against Iran](82533582.md), citing the need to prevent the regime from obtaining [nuclear weapons](21785.md) and to counter its regional influence.[^cite_note-338] On 24 March 2026, Australia finalised a [free trade agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_trade_agreements_of_Australia) with the [European Union](9317.md) (EU), concluding negotiations that had taken place between the two since June 2018.[^cite_note-339]

Albanese has described his political views as [progressive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressivism),[^cite_note-340] and is aligned with the [Labor Left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_Left) faction within the [Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Labor_Party).[^cite_note-Wanna-2019-341][^cite_note-342] Once described as "a leading figure of NSW Labor's minority Hard Left faction",[^cite_note-ABCcentrism-7] several journalists and analysts have noted his ideological shift toward [centrism](39619035.md) since becoming Labor leader.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346] In May 2025, he described his ideology as "progressive patriotism",[^cite_note-347] and has further explained his government as occupying the "radical centre".[^cite_note-ABCcentrism-7]

Albanese is a [republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republicanism_in_Australia), and supports replacing Australia's current [constitutional monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Australia).[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349] In a debate to mark the [Queen's Platinum Jubilee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platinum_Jubilee_of_Elizabeth_II#Australia), he told the Australian Parliament, "Even many Australians who do not hold with the principle of monarchy feel regard for her. You can be a republican, as I am, and still have the deepest respect for the Queen. She has done her duty with fidelity, integrity, humanity and, as she sometimes lets slip, a sly sense of humour."[^cite_note-350] He stated his desire to give [constitutional recognition](65267019.md) to [Indigenous Australians](12598742.md), and pledged to hold a referendum regarding an [Indigenous Voice to Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_Voice_to_Parliament) upon becoming prime minister.[^cite_note-351] He stated that this recognition should come before any referendum regarding Australia's status as a constitutional monarchy vs. a republic.[^cite_note-Karp-2023-352] The [Indigenous Voice to Parliament referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_Voice_to_Parliament_referendum) occurred on 14 October 2023 and was defeated.[^cite_note-353][^cite_note-354] Following a meeting with King [Charles III](125248.md) in September 2025, he ruled out holding a republic referendum during his term as prime minister.[^cite_note-355]

### Environmental issues

While serving in the [Gillard government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gillard_government), Albanese supported the introduction of [carbon pricing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_pricing),[^cite_note-356] and voted, along with the rest of the Labor Party, to establish the *[Clean Energy Act 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clean_Energy_Act_2011)*, which instituted a [carbon pricing scheme in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_pricing_in_Australia).[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] After the [Abbott government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbott_government) abolished the scheme in July 2014,[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360] Albanese stated that carbon pricing was no longer needed, as "the circumstances have changed".[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362]

Albanese is a prominent backer of [renewable energy in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Australia) and has declared that the country's "long-term future lies in renewable energy sources".[^cite_note-363] Upon his election in 2022, he said he would "end the [climate wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_wars)" and mitigation and policies to address [climate change in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Australia) would be a priority for his government, in contrast with those preceding it.[^cite_note-364] Albanese supported the introduction and sale of [electric vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plug-in_electric_vehicles_in_Australia).[^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366]

### Foreign policy

- Albanese with Japanese Prime Minister Fumio Kishida in 2023

  Albanese with [Japanese Prime Minister](24833.md) [Fumio Kishida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fumio_Kishida) in 2023

- Albanese with European Commission President Ursula von der Leyen at the NATO Summit in Vilnius, Lithuania, in 2023

  Albanese with European Commission President [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) at the [NATO](21133.md) Summit in [Vilnius](32597.md), Lithuania, in 2023

Albanese's foreign policy beliefs have been the subject of media attention. Writing for the *[Australian Financial Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Financial_Review)*, James Curran noted his shift from an "idealistic left-wing critic of globalisation" to "a true believer in the American alliance".[^cite_note-367] Albanese was staunchly opposed to the US-led [invasion of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_Iraq), saying in February 2003 that "Whatever criticisms can be made of the Iraqi regime, Islamic fundamentalism is not one of them. This is one of the reasons the United States supported Saddam Hussein in the 1980s, including supplying his regime with weapons of mass destruction, which he then used against both the Iranians and the Kurds."[^cite_note-368] Albanese repeatedly urged for the release of Australian whistleblower and [WikiLeaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WikiLeaks) founder [Julian Assange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_Assange), who was being held in custody in the United Kingdom [until 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_Assange#Plea_bargain_and_release).[^cite_note-369]

In February 2022, Albanese condemned [Russia's invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia's_invasion_of_Ukraine), remarking in a press release that it was a "grave moment for humanity".[^cite_note-370]

In May 2022, Albanese said Australia's relationship with [China](5405.md) would remain "a difficult one".[^cite_note-371] He said that "Australia values human rights. We have spoken out about the [treatment of Uyghurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Uyghurs_in_China), about what's occurred in Hong Kong, about Taiwan, about other minorities including in Tibet, that are suffering from human rights abuses."[^cite_note-372]

#### Israel–Palestine conflict

Albanese's views on the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md) have been the subject of media interest, with *[The Times of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_Israel)* categorising him as a supporter of Palestine.[^cite_note-TOI-373] Alongside [Joe Hockey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Hockey), Albanese established the parliamentary "Friends of Palestine" group in 1998.[^cite_note-374] Despite this, he has been a critic of the [Boycott, Divestment and Sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boycott,_Divestment_and_Sanctions) movement, calling it "clumsy and counterproductive".[^cite_note-TOI-373] During the [2014 Gaza War](43243199.md), he called Israel's bombardment of the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) a "[collective punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_punishment)" that was "completely unacceptable".[^cite_note-375] In 2018, he challenged a decision by the Australian government to vote against a UN human rights council motion calling for an investigation into the killings of Palestinian protesters during the [Great March of Return](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_March_of_Return). Shortly before the 2022 election, Albanese told *[The Australian Jewish News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Australian_Jewish_News)* that any decision he takes on Israel-Palestine will contribute "to a peaceful resolution of the conflict and to progress towards a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution)". When asked about a 2018 resolution to [recognise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_the_State_of_Palestine) the [State of Palestine](241405.md), Albanese insisted the motion "has no greater or lesser weight" than it did previously.[^cite_note-376] In October 2022, his government reversed the Morrison government's decision to recognise [West Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Jerusalem) as Israel's capital.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378] In December 2023, Albanese signed a joint statement, along with the prime ministers of Canada and New Zealand, calling for a ceasefire in the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380] Albanese has been criticised by conservative and pro-Israel opponents for his perceived inaction in combatting [antisemitism](1078.md) during his term.[^cite_note-381] In December 2024, Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) linked the [firebombing of a Melbourne synagogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Melbourne_synagogue_attack) to Albanese's "extreme anti-Israel position", which included supporting [United Nations resolutions](1437212.md) critical of Israel's conduct in the Palestinian territories,[^cite_note-382] and also accused his government of "pouring fuel on the antisemitic fire" following the [2025 Bondi Beach shooting](81839099.md).[^cite_note-383]

### Social issues



Albanese at the [Sydney Gay and Lesbian Mardi Gras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Gay_and_Lesbian_Mardi_Gras) in 2003

Albanese supports [abortion rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion_rights), stating in an interview in August 2019 that he believes "women do have a right to choose".[^cite_note-384] He is also in favour of legalising [voluntary euthanasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voluntary_euthanasia);[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386] in December 2022, his government repealed the *[Euthanasia Laws Act 1997](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euthanasia_Laws_Act_1997)*, which prevented the [territories of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territories_of_Australia) from legalising euthanasia.[^cite_note-387]

Albanese is a supporter and advocate for [LGBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT) rights,[^cite_note-388] and has regularly participated in the [Sydney Gay and Lesbian Mardi Gras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Gay_and_Lesbian_Mardi_Gras) since 1983.[^cite_note-Wanna-2019-341][^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390] When Labor Party members were granted a [conscience vote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscience_vote) on the *Marriage Amendment Bill 2012*, which would have legalised same-sex marriage in Australia, Albanese voted in favour of the bill, which was unsuccessful.[^cite_note-391] He opposed [holding a plebiscite for same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Marriage_Law_Postal_Survey), stating that "we shouldn't be having a public vote where we get to judge other families".[^cite_note-392] In 2017, Albanese also voted in favour of the *[Marriage Amendment (Definition and Religious Freedoms) Act 2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marriage_Amendment_\(Definition_and_Religious_Freedoms\)_Act_2017)*, the bill which ultimately legalised same-sex marriage.[^cite_note-393] Albanese has voiced support of [transgender rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transgender_rights_in_Australia), saying that "people coming to terms with their identity and who they are, I think that they need to be respected",[^cite_note-Karp-2023-352] although has voted against bills and motions intended to improve transgender rights in the past.[^cite_note-394]

Early in his political career, Albanese supported [drug decriminalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_decriminalisation), telling Parliament in 1997 that "drug use by individuals is a health issue, not a criminal issue".[^cite_note-395] However, in February 2022, he declined to commit to decriminalisation of [hard drugs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_drugs), commenting that the "current settings are appropriate".[^cite_note-396]

In July 2015, Albanese stated his opposition to the government's policy of turning back [asylum seekers](276005.md) who arrive to the country via boat, saying: "I couldn't ask someone else to do something that I couldn't see myself doing ... if people were in a boat including families and children, I myself couldn't turn that around."[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398] During the [2022 federal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Australian_federal_election) campaign, Albanese clarified that boat turnbacks would be incorporated into his government's policy,[^cite_note-399] leading to some critics accusing him of "flip-flopping" on the issue.[^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401] In August 2021, after the [Taliban seized control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Taliban_offensive) of [Afghanistan](737.md), Albanese urged the [Morrison government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morrison_government) to give [Afghan refugees](3657017.md) permanent residency in Australia.[^cite_note-402]

In May 2024, Albanese voiced his support for raising the age at which a child can open a [social media](5897742.md) account from 13 to 16, citing concerns over the mental health of young people, with his government passing legislation to compel social media companies to do so.[^cite_note-403] He has referred to social media as a "scourge".[^cite_note-404]

## Personal life

In 2000, Albanese married [Carmel Tebbutt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmel_Tebbutt), a future [Deputy Premier of New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_Premier_of_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406] They had met in Young Labor during the late 1980s,[^cite_note-407] and have one son together.[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409] The two separated in January 2019.[^cite_note-410] In June 2020, it was reported that Albanese was in a relationship[^cite_note-411] with [Jodie Haydon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jodie_Haydon).[^cite_note-412] Albanese said they had met at a dinner event in Melbourne a year after his separation from Tebbutt.[^cite_note-413] Albanese is the first divorcee to be appointed prime minister.[^cite_note-414] In February 2024, Albanese announced his engagement to Haydon after proposing to her at [The Lodge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Lodge,_Australia) in [Canberra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canberra).[^cite_note-415] On 29 November 2025, Albanese and Haydon were married in a private ceremony at The Lodge. Albanese is the first prime minister of Australia to be engaged or wed while in office.[^cite_note-416]

Albanese describes himself as "half-Italian and half-Irish"[^cite_note-417] and a "non-practising Catholic".[^cite_note-418] He is also a music fan who, not long after becoming prime minister, attended a [Gang of Youths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gang_of_Youths) concert at the [Enmore Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enmore_Theatre)[^cite_note-419] and previously intervened as transport minister to save a [Dolly Parton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolly_Parton) tour from bureaucratic red tape.[^cite_note-420] In 2013, he co-hosted a pre-election special of music program *[Rage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rage_\(TV_program\))* and his song selection included the [Pixies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pixies_\(band\)), [the Pogues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pogues), [the Smiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Smiths), [the Triffids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Triffids), [PJ Harvey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PJ_Harvey), [Nirvana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nirvana_\(band\)), [Hunters & Collectors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunters_&_Collectors) and [Joy Division](16579.md).[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422] On 30 November 2023, Albanese posted his [Spotify Wrapped](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spotify_Wrapped) to his [Instagram story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instagram_story), indicating his top artists for the year to be [Lana Del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lana_Del_Rey), [Taylor Swift](5422144.md), [Hilltop Hoods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilltop_Hoods), [Bruce Springsteen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Springsteen), and [Lily Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lily_Allen).[^cite_note-423] In January 2026, he posted his picks for Triple j's Hottest 100 of 2025, among of which included [Olivia Dean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivia_Dean), [Hatchie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hatchie), Hilltop Hoods (again), [Kita Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kita_Alexander), Lily Allen (again), [Ocean Alley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocean_Alley), [Ruby Fields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruby_Fields), [Spacey Jane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spacey_Jane), [G Flip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G_Flip) and [Wolf Alice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolf_Alice).[^cite_note-424]

As a lifelong supporter of the [South Sydney Rabbitohs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Sydney_Rabbitohs) rugby league club, Albanese was a board member of the club from 1999 to 2002 and influential in the fight to have the club readmitted to the [National Rugby League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rugby_League) (NRL) competition.[^cite_note-Monahan-2013-425] During October 2009, *[The Sydney Morning Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sydney_Morning_Herald)* reported that Albanese had opposed an attempt to appoint the former Liberal prime minister [John Howard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Howard) to a senior position in the NRL. Albanese stated he had phoned the NRL chief executive, [David Gallop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Gallop), as well as other league officials, to advise them against the idea. He then implored officials at Souths to help stop the suggestion from gaining momentum.[^cite_note-426] In 2013, he was made a life member of the club.[^cite_note-Monahan-2013-425] He is also a fan of [Australian rules football](2403.md), and supports the [Hawthorn Football Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawthorn_Football_Club),[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428] with Albanese attending the [1991 AFL Grand Final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_AFL_Grand_Final) which saw Hawthorn claim its 9th premiership.[^cite_note-429]

Albanese was injured in a [side collision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Side_collision) while driving in [Marrickville, New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marrickville,_New_South_Wales), on 8 January 2021. He underwent treatment at [Royal Prince Alfred Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Prince_Alfred_Hospital) and was reportedly "injured externally and internally and had suffered considerable shock in the immediate aftermath of the impact". The other driver was a 17-year-old who was charged with negligent driving.[^cite_note-430] Emergency workers told Albanese that if the teen's car had hit just 30 centimetres (12 in) either side of where it did, Albanese "would almost certainly have been killed".[^cite_note-431] Shortly following this accident, Albanese lost over 18 kilograms (40 lb) by cutting out carbohydrates and reducing his alcohol intake, in an effort to be "match fit" for his election campaign.[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433] Four years after the crash, Albanese revealed that he still suffers ongoing spinal issues and has difficulty sitting in a car for long distances.[^cite_note-434]

Prior to 2024, Albanese owned multiple investment and residential properties in the [Inner West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inner_West) of Sydney and Canberra.[^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436] In 2024, Albanese and Haydon purchased a beachside cliff-top mansion in the [Central Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Coast_\(New_South_Wales\)) town of [Copacabana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copacabana,_New_South_Wales) for \$4.3M.[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439] In October 2024, [REA Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/REA_Group) estimated his property portfolio at \$8.8 million.[^cite_note-440]
